| PRED-01 | **Recorded before the m11 post-guard arm existed** (job 25681998 was still running m10 at 70/114). If the post-COMPLETE executor actions were producing `prefix_m11`'s excess over the planner, then silencing them should pull the arm toward the planner on exactly the prefix-exhausted episodes: 60 of 114 pairs at +1.78 pp gives an expected overall fall of ~0.94 pp, i.e. m11 post-guard ≈ **0.8213** against 0.8307 pre-guard. A fall materially larger than that says the guard removed more than the bonus; a fall near zero says the prefix alone was already carrying those episodes and the "bonus" was never doing work [OBSERVED campaign/results/hj12_unified_frontier_20260922.report.json: key "noninferiority.arms.prefix_m11.goal_pass_no_handoff" arm_score 0.868483, reference_score 0.8507, n_pairs 60] [INFERRED]. | `campaign/results/hj12_unified_frontier_20260922.report.json` | `noninferiority.arms.prefix_m11.goal_pass_no_handoff` | exploratory | prefix curve |
| SHAPE-01 | Post-guard, all-episodes `goal_pass_rate` is **monotone increasing** across the completed grid: 0.6856, 0.7190, 0.7237, 0.7544, 0.7627, 0.7852, 0.8065 at m = 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10. The pre-guard curve was not: it dipped at m6 (0.7340 to 0.7145) and again at m10 (0.8134 to 0.7906). The terminal guard therefore removed the m9 to m10 reversal that the "shape is not established" objection rested on [OBSERVED /scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj12_prefix_m*_{20260922,20260923}/prefix_handoff result.json `goal_pass_rate`]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj12_prefix_m*_{20260922,20260923}` | `result.json` `goal_pass_rate` | exploratory | prefix curve |
| SHAPE-02 | On the **fixed** population of the 73 episodes where the post-guard m10 prefix genuinely handed off — so every arm in the comparison is a real handoff and the population never changes — the curve is flat through m8 and then rises: 0.7017, 0.6927, 0.6974, 0.7012, 0.6891, 0.7174, 0.7506 at m = 2, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10. The m2 to m8 spread is 1.26 pp, well inside the 3.31 pp replicate floor (NOISE-01); the m8 to m10 rise is **+6.15 pp**, which clears it. Pinning to the largest completed m makes the key sets nested, which is the correct control because whether a prefix already finished the task is deterministic given (source trajectory, m) [OBSERVED same result.json set, joined on `task_id`+`seed` filtered by `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` > 0 at m10]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj12_prefix_m*_20260923` | `result.json` `goal_pass_rate`, `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` | exploratory | prefix curve |
| MIX-01 | The all-episodes rise from m=2 to m=10 is +12.09 pp (0.6856 to 0.8065) and decomposes exactly into two disjoint parts. On the 73 episodes m10 hands off (64.0% weight): 0.7017 to 0.7506 = +4.89 pp, contributing **+3.13 pp**. On the 41 episodes the m10 prefix completes alone (36.0% weight): 0.6568 to 0.9059 = +24.91 pp, contributing **+8.96 pp**. Sum 12.09 pp. So **74% of the headline rise is the replayed prefix finishing the task itself**, and 26% is the executor finishing better from a deeper prefix. Both are real; only the second is a statement about handoff, and the first must never be reported as one [OBSERVED same result.json set, split on `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` at m10]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj12_prefix_m{2,10}_20260923` | `result.json` `goal_pass_rate`, `totals.per_actor.executor.n_calls` | exploratory | prefix curve |

| NOISE-05 | **The registered replicate floor, first half.** `docs/prereg_hj13_shape_20260923.md` §3 (:114-117) requires `prefix_m6` and `prefix_m9` re-run once more under identical settings in a single job; that job is PBS 25693138 (`ARMS="hj12_prefix_m6 hj12_prefix_m9"`, `DATE=20260923rep`, same frozen configs and the same post-guard code as the originals). Observations are never emitted by the executor: across every episode of `hj13_prefix_zs_m9_20260923/prefix_handoff/1` the only event/actor pairs present are `action|executor`, `observation|environment`, `evaluate|environment`, and `run_start`/`run_end`/`report` with actor `system`. The true error signal on that arm is the observation text prefix (167 observations begin `Execution failed. Traceback:`, 52 begin `Execution successful.`) with `error_type` null throughout, so the text test is load-bearing and `error_type` alone is insufficient. **M1 (MECH-08) and M3 (MECH-10) are unaffected**: M1 reads only source planner trajectories and M3 reads the handoff flag and goal-pass values, neither of which touches this branch. | GUARD-03 | **The X33d fatal guard did its job on its first run, and revealed a second fact.** With the empty-population and divergence checks in place, the zero-shot receiver completed and the tailored receiver **aborted**: `Fatal: arm 'm7' divergence count 8/114 (7.02%) exceeds 5% threshold`. That arm is the *comparison* arm `hj12_prefix_m7_20260922`, a pre-F0(a) run in which the live loop still let the executor act after a replayed `COMPLETE`. Those episodes therefore legitimately carry `handoff_occurred = False` together with executor `n_calls > 0` — the documented pre-repair behaviour, not a data defect. So the blanket 5% threshold is mis-specified: the direction that is genuinely impossible is `handoff_occurred = True` with zero executor calls. X33e replaces the blanket check with that directional one (fatal at any count above zero), keeps the missing-`report`-event and empty-population checks fatal, and records the benign direction in a new `post_complete_executor_actions` field. Recorded because it is the concrete instance justifying the "make an empty population fatal" rule that MECH-09 motivated: the guard cost one aborted run and bought two real findings [OBSERVED the STEP-2 stdout of the 19:40 PBS run; OBSERVED docs/claims_ledger.md MECH-09; OBSERVED campaign/RUNS.md §14 for the post-COMPLETE behaviour]. | `campaign/results/hj13_mechanism_zeroshot_20260923b.report.json` | stdout of the 2026-09-22 19:40 run | process record | mechanism |
| CEIL-04 | **Direct evidence for the over-acting reading of CEIL-07.** Comparing the two planner samples' recorded trajectory depth over all 114 episodes each: the cap-81 sample (`hj13_planner_alone_cap81_20260923`) has **mean 16.35 actions per episode** (min 6, p25 9, median 12, p75 16, max 40) against the cap-25 sample's (`hj1b_planner_20260915`) **mean 13.42** (min 5, p25 9, median 12, p75 16, max 24). The distributions are identical through the median and diverge only in the upper tail, which is exactly where the higher cap binds: the cap-81 planner keeps acting on the episodes the cap-25 planner was cut off in, and it scores 6.47 pp *lower* (CEIL-07). This favours reading (a) of CEIL-07 — later actions damaging partially-correct state — over reading (b), run-to-run drift, because drift would not concentrate in the tail. It does not eliminate (b). Incidentally this also establishes that the cap-81 sample is usable as a replay source at every registered depth: **114/114 episodes have ≥6 actions, 99/114 have ≥9, 76/114 have ≥11**, against 112/94/74 for cap-25 [OBSERVED action counts computed by `jq 'select(.event_type=="action")'` over every `events.jsonl` of both campaigns, 2026-09-22 ~19:52]. | `/scratch/n12194778/sidekick/results/hj13_planner_alone_cap81_20260923/planner_alone` | per-episode `action` event counts | exploratory (H0) | frontier |

| MECH-05 | **M2, valid: a deeper prefix roughly halves the chance the executor errors at all. This is the compounding-error mechanism, measured.** With the actor filter corrected (X33e), the share of handoff episodes in which the executor hits at least one failed observation falls monotonically with depth. **Tailored receiver** (`hj12_prefix_m*_20260923`, handoff episodes in brackets): m=2 **85.09%** [114], m=4 75.44% [114], m=6 56.76% [111], m=7 46.23% [106], m=8 48.39% [93], m=9 **42.68%** [82], m=10 40.85% [71], m=11 42.59% [54]. **Untailored receiver** (`hj13_prefix_zs_m*_20260923`): m=6 **80.18%** [111], m=9 64.63% [82], m=11 61.11% [54]. Both receivers fall by roughly a third to a half over the measured range, and the untailored executor errors far more often than the tailored one at every depth (80.2% vs 56.8% at m=6; 64.6% vs 42.7% at m=9), which is the error-side signature of tailoring. Conditional on erroring at all, the tailored executor's first error moves *earlier* relative to handoff as depth grows — share of first errors at the very first executor step rises 20.62% (m=2) → 39.68% (m=6) → 40.00% (m=9) → 51.72% (m=10), and within the first two steps 41.24% → 65.71% → 72.41%. Reading: depth does not make the executor better at recovering from errors; it reduces how often it is put in a position to err, and what remains is concentrated immediately after the handoff, i.e. at the seam. The untailored receiver shows no such shift (first-step share flat at 47–49% across all three depths), so the seam concentration is a property of the tailored receiver. ⚠ Descriptive, on unequal populations: the handoff population shrinks with depth (114 → 54), so these rates are conditional on handing off at that depth and are **not** paired contrasts; no CI is attached and none should be quoted [OBSERVED campaign/results/hj13_mechanism_tailored_20260923c.report.json and …_zeroshot_…: keys "m2_compounding_error.primary.m<N>.{error_rate_on_handoff,share_first_error_at_step_1,share_first_error_in_steps_1_2,handoff_episodes,silenced_episodes}"]. | `campaign/results/hj13_mechanism_tailored_20260923c.report.json` | `m2_compounding_error.primary.m9.error_rate_on_handoff` | exploratory (F3) | mechanism |

| QWEN-02 | **⚠ The Qwen floor is confounded by output-format acquisition and must never be used as the denominator for a "the prefix lifts Qwen by X" claim.** The zero-shot Qwen3-8B floor arms score `executor_alone` **0.2481** and `prompt_only` **0.2690**, both with **TGC exactly 0.0000** and `error_type: limit` in **114/114** and **67/67** episodes respectively — every episode exhausted the 40-step budget. A census of `action|executor` events with `payload.kind == "COMPLETE"` across the whole campaign shows why: **Qwen emits the completion action 0 times in 114 `executor_alone` episodes and 0 times in 80 `prompt_only` episodes, but 32 times (32 episodes) at prefix m=6 and 37 times at m=9.** The granite base receiver, under the **identical** harness, prompt structure and stop tokens (`["</py>", "</python>", "</tool_call>"]`, verified byte-identical in `configs/hj1r_prompt_only.yaml:26`, `configs/hj15_prompt_only_zsq.yaml:30`, `configs/hj15_prefix_zsq_m9.yaml:33`), completes in **53 of 114** `prompt_only` episodes and 61 of 114 at prefix m=9. So this is not a stop-token truncation and not a template defect: **Qwen3-8B does not produce the harness's terminal action at all until it has seen the planner's recorded trajectory demonstrate the format in context.** Consequences: (1) part of any floor-relative Qwen lift is **in-context format acquisition, not task competence**, so statements of the form "the prefix takes Qwen from 0.25 to 0.70" are **not supported** and must not be made; (2) **QWEN-01 is unaffected** — the +25.25 pp m6→m9 contrast is between two arms that both carry a demonstrated trajectory, so the format confound is common to both and differences out; (3) the F5 second-family claim must be stated as *"within prefix arms, the depth effect replicates in a second executor family"*, with this row cited as the reason the family's absolute floor is not comparable to granite's; (4) a clean Qwen floor would need either a format demonstration added to the zero-shot prompt or a tailored Qwen adapter (F5 full), and neither exists yet. | HF-01 | **C3, the suffix-trained adapter: handoff training raises the shallow end and flattens the curve; it does not move the threshold outward.** The adapter `sft_b_plus_handoff_granite8b` — trained on the recorded planner trajectories cut at the same depths the serving path uses, and verified healthy in HF-TRAIN-01 — was evaluated on the identical replayed `hj1b` prefixes at m ∈ {6, 9, 11} (PBS 25699981, 114/114 episodes per arm, 4/6/5 `limit`, 0 crash). Point estimates beside the two existing receivers: **m=6 0.7480** (standard adapter 0.7237, untailored 0.6825); **m=9 0.7840** (0.7852, 0.7845); **m=11 0.8033** (0.8098, 0.8345). TGC 0.4035 / 0.5351 / 0.5877. So the handoff adapter is **+2.43 pp over the standard adapter at m=6, −0.12 pp at m=9 and −0.65 pp at m=11**, and it is **below the untailored receiver at m=11 by 3.12 pp**. Its own curve rises 0.7480 → 0.8033, a span of 5.53 pp, against the standard adapter's 8.61 pp and the untailored receiver's 15.20 pp over the same depths. Reading, consistent with TAILOR-07 and MECH-07: **prefix depth, executor tailoring and handoff-suffix training are partially substitutable and do not stack.** Each one raises the shallow end; whichever is applied first claims most of the available gain, and the receiver that starts lowest (untailored, no suffix training) has the most room and therefore the steepest curve. The rise exists only across a span of steps.** Red-team attack 6's multiplicity limb held that eight adjacent-m comparisons were reported uncorrected, so one "significant" step was expected by chance. | COST-01 | **On the registered matched-trigger pair the action channel strictly dominates: it is better AND cheaper on all three cost axes simultaneously. This is the paper's strongest single sentence.** Against `advise_fixed_k_10_fullctx` — the comparator matched on trigger and on full context — `takeover_fixed_k_10` is cheaper on **non-cached tokens** (41,464 vs 49,819 per episode), on **provider dollars** ($0.004820 vs $0.005494) and on **hosted calls** (2.316 vs 2.465), while scoring **+6.69 pp** higher on `goal_pass` (CHAN-C1-02). There is no cost axis on which advice buys back its quality deficit, so the channel claim needs no "at a price" qualifier against its matched comparator. Takeover uses fewer non-cached tokens (41,464 vs 43,823) and fewer hosted calls (2.32 vs 2.42) but costs **more** in provider dollars ($0.004820 vs $0.004386), so the two arms swap rank between the token axis and the dollar axis, and swap back between the dollar axis and the call axis. ⚠ The *mechanism* — that takeover pays for more expensively-billed output/reasoning tokens while starved advice reads a short context and so bills mostly cached input — is **[INFERRED]** from the price card's rate structure and has not been confirmed against a per-token-class breakdown; do not state it as observed. (1) The flip does **not** touch the frontier ordering that the thesis rests on: `executor_alone` < `plan_only` < the two k=10 arms < `advise_k3_starved` < `prefix_m6` < `prefix_m9` < `prefix_m11` < `ceiling_cap25` < `ceiling_cap81` holds on all three axes. (2) It does **not** weaken COST-01, because the matched comparator is `advise_k10_fullctx`, against which takeover is cheaper on every axis; the starved arm is a legacy context-limited configuration retained only for continuity with the registered H0 set. | NARR-01 | **The replayed prefix works by what it TELLS the executor, not by the environment state it leaves behind: rendering the planner's actions as plain text into a FRESH environment is statistically indistinguishable from actually having executed them, on both receivers and both metrics.** ⚠⚠ **SCOPE, added after this row was written: everything below is measured at m = 9 ONLY, and m = 9 is the depth at which the narrated and executed curves are CLOSEST.** The narrated curve has since completed at m = 6 and m = 11, where **execution beats narration significantly on both clusterings** (TGC −13.16 pp, scenario [−21.05, −5.26], at m = 6; goal_pass −6.58 pp [−9.98, −3.64] and TGC −11.40 pp [−22.81, −2.63] at m = 11) (goal_pass narrated vs executed: 0.6128 vs 0.6825 at m = 6, 0.7676 vs 0.7845 at m = 9, 0.7686 vs 0.8345 at m = 11). See NARR-03. Quoting this row without its depth qualifier overstates it. The narrated arm takes the planner's first 9 recorded actions, renders them as **text** appended to `plan_steps`, and runs the executor from **step 0 in a fresh environment** — information-matched to the executed prefix, state-unmatched. Executed minus narrated at m = 9, n = 114 paired: **tailored** receiver `goal_pass` **+1.84 pp**, scenario [−2.67, +6.76], task [−3.70, +7.75]; tailored TGC **+3.51 pp**, scenario [−5.26, +11.40], task [−4.39, +11.40]; **untailored** receiver `goal_pass` **+1.69 pp**, scenario [−4.16, +7.11], task [−3.70, +7.38]; untailored TGC **+6.14 pp**, scenario [−2.63, +14.91], task [−1.75, +14.91]. **All eight intervals include zero.** Arms: tailored executed 0.7852 / narrated 0.7667 / one-plan floor 0.7181; untailored executed 0.7845 / narrated 0.7676 / one-plan floor 0.2885. So the carried environment state — the files written, the APIs already called, the variables already bound — contributes nothing resolvable at n = 114 once the *same information* reaches the executor as text. ⚠ This is an equivalence-shaped reading of intervals that include zero, so it must be stated as *"we cannot distinguish them at n = 114"*, never as "narration is as good as execution"; the intervals are ±5–15 pp wide and would not exclude a real 5 pp advantage for execution, and the TGC intervals are the wider pair, consistently positive in point estimate (+3.51, +6.14), which is the direction one would expect if state helps a little. ⚠ The ± observations variants (narration with the environment's responses included) were still running when this row was written and are **not** part of it [OBSERVED campaign/results/hj16_narrated_tailored_complete_20260923.report.json and hj16_narrated_untailored_complete_20260923.report.json: keys `arms.*.{goal_pass_all,tgc_all,n}`, `contrasts.{goal_pass_all,tgc_all}_executed_m9_minus_narrated_m9`, `…_executed_zs_m9_minus_narrated_zs_m9`, each with `ci95_pp_scenario` and `ci95_pp_task`]. | `campaign/results/hj16_narrated_untailored_complete_20260923.report.json` | `contrasts.goal_pass_all_executed_zs_m9_minus_narrated_zs_m9` | **registered (X38)** | mechanism |

That is the direct measurement behind QWEN-06's reading that this family acquires the convention in context and not from fine-tuning. That self-report was false. Audited against raw per-episode `result.json`: `advise_fixed_k_10` starved TGC given as **0.3947**, true value **0.4123** — and 0.3947 is a verbatim **fill-down of `plan_only`'s TGC on the row immediately above**; `advise_fixed_k_3` given as **0.3509**, true value **0.4561**, an error of **10.52 pp**; `prefix_m6` given as **0.4211**, true value **0.4298**. All three are now corrected in `paper/preprint_dev_20260923.md` and their verified values are recorded here as the source they lacked: `advise_k10_starved` TGC **0.4123**, `advise_k3_starved` TGC **0.4561**, `prefix_m6` TGC **0.4298**, all n = 114. **The transferable lesson is about the audit, not the worker**: an instruction to write `[TODO:` when a source is missing is *not self-enforcing*, because a model that does not know it is guessing cannot comply with it. What does enforce it is a **set-difference check** — extract every numeric literal from the draft and assert each appears in the cited source — which found all three in a single command and would have found them whatever the worker claimed. Run that check on every generated document that carries numbers, and treat any self-reported "zero unsourced claims" as unevidenced. (2) **The narration really is the planner's own actions.** The packet's `plan` event carries **14** `plan_steps`: the 5 original plan steps followed by **9** appended entries, each labelled *"Expert trajectory, step N of 9 (code the planner actually ran on this task)"* and containing the verbatim Python the planner executed — including the concrete API signatures, the argument values and the login credentials it used. So "information-matched to m = 9" is literal, not approximate. (3) **Both receivers read the same file.** `hj16_narrated_m9_bplus.yaml` and `hj16_narrated_m9_zs.yaml` both point at `packet_source: /scratch/n12194778/sidekick/artifacts/packets/hj16_narrated_m9`, so the tailored/untailored contrast varies the receiver and nothing else. (4) **The phantom-alias trap is avoided.** The untailored config sets `lora_name: null` and runs under **`prompt_only`**, with an in-file comment naming the hazard (`sft_plan.py:18` defaults `adapter_name` to `sft_plan` when `lora_name` is null, `runner.py:256-258`); had it run under `sft_plan` it would have silently served the BASE model while reporting the alias. Its results path `hj16_narrated_m9_zs_20260923/prompt_only` confirms it did not. ⚠ A mechanistic consequence worth stating in the paper rather than burying: part of what the narrated prefix conveys is **credentials and exact call signatures**, not merely strategy, which is the same thing MECH-08's front-loaded-discovery finding says from the other direction. | DID-01 | **🔺 UPGRADES the paper's Limitation 5 from a declined claim to a measured one, at the deep end only: receiver tailoring significantly changes how well narration substitutes for execution at m = 11.** The preprint currently states that its mechanism reading "rests on comparing *which* paired contrasts resolve on each receiver, not on a difference-in-differences" and that "the DiD carries no interval in our analysis". It now does. Forming `(narrated − executed)_tailored − (narrated − executed)_untailored` **per episode** and bootstrapping clusters of that single series (10,000 resamples, seed 20260924, n = 114 pairs, 0 episodes dropped, 0 crashes in any of the four arms): m = 6 **+4.45 pp** scenario [−6.71, +14.25] (task [−5.07, +13.98]); m = 9 **−0.16 pp** [−8.36, +7.11] (task [−8.51, +8.04]); **m = 11 +6.09 pp scenario [+1.56, +11.00], task [+0.63, +11.85] — excludes zero on BOTH clusterings**, and TGC agrees at **+12.28 pp** scenario [+0.88, +26.32], task [+2.63, +22.81]. The component gaps at m = 11 are −0.49 pp on the tailored receiver (narration ≈ execution) against −6.58 pp on the untailored one (execution wins), from arm means 0.804886/0.809825 and 0.768640/0.834456 — the latter two reconciling exactly with SHAPE-06 and CHAN-ZS-04. ⚠ Multiplicity: three depths were tested on two metrics and only m = 11 resolves, so this is **exploratory, not confirmatory** — report it as "resolved at m = 11" and never as "the interaction holds", and note that the m = 6 point estimate has the same sign while m = 9 sits on zero. **A9 (2026-09-23) →** ROB-08: the `goal_pass` interaction at m = 11 survives an exact scenario sign-flip test (p = 0.0235), but the TGC corroboration does not (p = 0.0996), so "TGC agrees" must carry that qualifier. | `campaign/results/j14_did_narration_receiver_20260924.report.json` | `dids.m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp`, `dids.m11.tgc.scenario.ci95_pp` | exploratory, interaction | f6/f7 |

| C81-01 | **The prefix curve replicates on an independent planner sample, and its endpoint is indistinguishable from the original sample's despite the source planner scoring significantly worse.** Six arms replay the **cap-81** planner trajectories (`hj13_planner_alone_cap81_20260923`, 0.7637) instead of the cap-25 ones (`hj1b_planner_20260915`, 0.8284) that every other prefix arm in this thesis replays; the untailored three are complete. Curve: `goal_pass` **0.7201 → 0.7650 → 0.7932** and TGC **0.4561 → 0.5263 → 0.5526** at m = 6/9/11, n = 114 each. Over the span m = 6 → m = 11 the rise is **+7.31 pp** `goal_pass`, scenario [+2.07, +12.80], and **+9.65 pp** TGC, scenario [+2.63, +16.67] — **both exclude zero**. ⚠ As with the cap-25 curve and exactly as MULT-01 requires, **no single step is individually significant**: m = 6 → m = 9 is +4.49 `goal_pass` [−1.72, +11.29] (TGC +7.02 [+0.88, +13.16] is the lone exception) and m = 9 → m = 11 is +2.82 [−1.55, +7.16] (TGC +2.63 [−3.51, +9.65]). Report the span, never a step. **The generality result**: at m = 11 the cap-81 prefix scores **−4.13 pp** against the cap-25 prefix, scenario [−8.84, +0.50], task [−9.74, +1.44] — **including zero**, so the two planner samples' prefix endpoints cannot be distinguished at n = 114, even though the cap-81 planner *itself* scores 6.47 pp below the cap-25 planner (CEIL-07). A weaker planner's recorded trajectory therefore yields a prefix of statistically indistinguishable value at depth 11, which is the strongest available answer to "the whole effect depends on one lucky planner run". ⚠ The curve is **compressed** on the weaker source — span 7.31 pp against the cap-25 receiver's 15.20 pp — and it starts higher (0.7201 against 0.6825 at m = 6), so the two curves converge rather than translate; do not describe this as "the same curve". ⚠ The tailored cap-81 arms were still running when this row was written [OBSERVED campaign/results/hj17_prefix_c81_zs_curve_20260923.report.json: keys `arms.c81_m{6,9,11}.{goal_pass_all,tgc_all}`, `contrasts.{goal_pass_all,tgc_all}_c81_m{6,9}_minus_c81_m{9,11}`, `contrasts.{goal_pass_all,tgc_all}_c81_m11_minus_c25_m11`]. | `campaign/results/hj17_prefix_c81_zs_curve_20260923.report.json` | `contrasts.goal_pass_all_c81_m11_minus_c25_m11` | exploratory (T4) | prefix curve |

That shape is now shown to be sample-specific. **Consequence for the write-up: "depth substitutes for tailoring" must be demoted from a finding to an observation made on one planner sample and not reproduced on a second.** The honest statement is that receiver tailoring and prefix depth both raise the shallow end, that no within-depth receiver gap is resolved on either sample, and that the ordering at the deep end is not stable across samples. ⚠ Do not read the non-replication as evidence *against* substitutability either; the intervals are ±5–11 pp wide and would not resolve the 2–4 pp effects at issue. | CHAN-PRICE-02 | **🔺 REGISTERED PREDICTION P4 FAILED, and the decision rule attached to it does not apply, because it failed in the opposite direction to the one the rule was written for. Recorded here rather than silently reinterpreted.** P4 predicted the arm's non-cached planner tokens per episode would land in **[300k, 700k]**, above `prefix_m9`'s ~357k. Observed: **1,414,410 non-cached tokens and 19.02 hosted calls per episode** — roughly **2.0× the top of the predicted range**, **3.2×** `prefix_m11`'s 443,361 tokens, **1.7×** its 11.25 calls, and **28.4×** `advise_fixed_k_10_fullctx`'s 49,819 tokens. So P4 fails on its stated range. **The prereg's remedy for a P4 failure is written for under-pricing**: *"If it lands below 300k the arm has not achieved matched pricing and P2 must be reported as not tested"*, and the §5 rule then says to report the arm as higher-frequency advice only and to state that *"advice remains unpriced at the action channel's budget."* Neither applies: the arm was priced far **above** the action channel, not below it, so the hazard the prediction guarded against — declaring a channel win that was really a budget win — is excluded *a fortiori*. P2 is therefore reported as **tested and supported**, and the correct statement is the stronger one: advice loses by 14.68 pp while consuming 3.2× the tokens and 1.7× the hosted calls. ⚠⚠ This is a **deviation from the literal text of a frozen prereg** and is flagged as such: the registered numeric range was missed, the registered remedy was judged inapplicable by its own stated logic, and a reader who disagrees with that judgement should read P2 as *"not tested at the registered budget"* and rely instead on COST-01, which establishes the channel result at **matched trigger and matched context** with no budget question at all. | POOL-01 | **A third planner seed lifts every cap-81 contrast from 114 to 171 paired episodes at zero hosted cost. It tightens every interval, it confirms the depth *span* on both receivers — and it does NOT resolve the tailored m9→m11 step, which sits on the decision boundary and flips with the bootstrap RNG.** The six HJ-18 seed-3 replay arms (`hj18_prefix_c81s3_{zs,bplus}_m{6,9,11}_20260924`) each completed **57/57 with zero crashes**, replaying the seed-3 planner sample recorded in CEIL-06; the only non-empty `error_type` is `limit` (untailored 10/3/1 and tailored 11/7/3 at m = 6/9/11), which is not a crash under the `j8_frontier` convention and which falls with depth exactly as MECH-10/07 predict. Pooled with their HJ-17 counterparts — episode keys are `(task_id, seed)` so the two campaigns cannot collide, all seven arms load **171** episodes over seeds [1, 2, 3], and **0 episodes are dropped** from any contrast. **What is established:** the span m6→m11 on both receivers — untailored **+7.6988 pp (7.70)** scenario [+3.6895, +12.0579], task [+3.1433, +12.3029]; tailored **+8.3912 pp** [+4.2023, +12.6287], task [+4.0912, +12.9632]. The depth **span** remains the only safe depth claim on either receiver, exactly as MULT-01 has required throughout. **A9 (2026-09-23) →** ROB-06: both spans survive the exact sign-flip and wild-bootstrap alternatives (scenario p = 0.0019 untailored, 0.0014 tailored), and the tailored m9→m11 step stays unresolved under both (p = 0.0749). | `campaign/results/j15_pooled_cap81_3seed_20260924.report.json` (two-seed baseline: `j15_unpooled_cap81_2seed_20260924.report.json`) | `contrasts.zs_depth_m6_m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp`, `contrasts.t_depth_m6_m11.goal_pass_rate.scenario.ci95_pp` | exploratory (T1 power); span established, m9→m11 step NOT established | f1 |

| POOL-04 | **🔺 METHOD, and a standing check for this project: an interval bound that sits within ~1 pp of its decision threshold must be re-run at several bootstrap RNG seeds before its verdict is reported, because at B = 10,000 the Monte Carlo error on a percentile bound is itself a few tenths of a point.** This was not a hypothetical. Pooling to 171 pairs produced a tailored m9→m11 interval whose lower bound cleared zero by **0.15 pp**, which would have been written up as "the third seed resolves the depth step". Re-running the identical estimand on the identical episodes gives, seed → lower bound, 20260924 → +0.1526, 1 → +0.0801, 2 → +0.0544, **3 → −0.0140**, 7 → +0.1468, 101 → +0.1953, 999 → +0.1088 [OBSERVED campaign/results/j15_bootstrap_stability_20260924.report.json: key `probes["pooled_t_depth_m9_m11.goal_pass.scenario_lower_bound"].by_seed`; the seed order in earlier versions of this row did not match that map and was corrected on 2026-09-23 (A9)] — **the verdict flips on 1 of 7 seeds (seed 3)** — and a 200,000-resample run gives +0.1041, so the estimand's true bound is ≈ +0.10 with a 10k-resample jitter of roughly ±0.1 pp straddling zero. Three other bounds were probed and all survived: DID-01's m = 11 interaction is strictly positive at every seed on both clusterings (scenario 1.46–1.56, task 0.45–0.63, 200k = +1.493 / +0.5921), and POOL-02's tailored-m11 task-clustered bound is negative at every seed (200k = −0.3906). A fourth is **stable at an atom**: the untailored m11 TGC upper bound is +7.0175 at every seed and at 200k, because TGC takes discrete values and the percentile lands on the same attainable one — stability there reflects discreteness, not precision, and it cannot adjudicate a 0.0175 pp gap against the 7.00 margin. **Standing rule adopted:** before any bound within 1 pp of zero or of the NI margin is reported as deciding anything, run the seven-seed probe and report the range; if the sign changes, report the quantity as unresolved. ⚠ Known outstanding application: CHAN-PRICE-01's P3 task-clustered upper bound of **+0.01 pp** is by far the thinnest margin in the ledger and has not yet been probed — it is computed by `j8_frontier`, not `j14_did`, so the probe needs a separate wiring; until then P3's task-clustered near-miss must keep the hedge already written into that row. **A9 (2026-09-23) →** ROB-04 closes the outstanding application: probed at the same seven seeds, the P3 task-clustered bound is negative at six of them, so the CHAN-PRICE-01 hedge is replaced rather than kept. | `campaign/results/j15_bootstrap_stability_20260924.report.json` | `probes.pooled_t_depth_m9_m11_goal_pass_scenario_lower_bound.by_seed` | method; supersedes any boundary reading of POOL-01 | — |

## ROB — inference and robustness (J16)

Merged 2026-09-23 (A9) from `campaign/results/j16_ledger_rows_inference_draft.md` (ROB-01..08) and
`campaign/results/j16_ledger_rows_robustness_draft.md` (ROB-10..20), text and keys as drafted; the
drafts' abbreviation `R` in the artifact column is written out. Key conventions. ROB-01..08:
`headline.rows["<row id>"]` is a JSON object key into `campaign/results/j16_inference_20260923.report.json`
(row ids contain dots, so the brackets are literal); the 19-scenario sign-flip p is **exact** (all 2^19
patterns enumerated), the 57-task p is Monte Carlo over 100,000 patterns, and wild intervals are B = 10,000
at seed 20260924. ROB-10..21: keys are jq-style paths into `campaign/results/j16_robustness_20260923.report.json`;
unless a row says otherwise, intervals are 95 % paired cluster-percentile bootstrap intervals, B = 10,000,
seed 20260924, resampling the 19 scenarios; pp values are arm minus comparator; episodes are paired by
(task_id, seed) over j10-clean rows. ROB-21 was added by A9 for F-e values the preprint cites that the
drafted rows do not carry.

The 10 re-run episodes (`result.json` mtimes 21:32–21:35, after the other 104) now sum to 2.31 `goal_pass` and 1 solved task, so its floor (0.1903 / 0.0175) is inflated by **at most +2.03 pp `goal_pass` and +0.88 pp TGC**; (c) for `hj1r_prompt_only_20260916`, one episode re-run of unknown prior type (`50e1ac9_2` s1, mtime 2026-09-17 01:07, now 0.5 / TGC 0) bounds the inflation of its floor (0.2885 / 0.0526) at **+0.44 pp `goal_pass`, 0 TGC**. ## J17 — dev review fixes: handoff-only depth, chord, Ganz metrics, cost share, limits, content, metrics, latency, BY-FDR, census (A10)

Added 2026-09-24 (A10, plan R7.7) for the numbers Paper A v2 quotes from two committed dev reports:
`campaign/results/j17_depth_fixes_20260924.report.json` (built by `scripts/analysis/j17_depth_fixes.py`; HO-*,
HO-NI-*, CHORD-*, GANZ-*, COST-04) and `campaign/results/j17_channel_fixes_20260924.report.json` (built by
`scripts/analysis/j17_channel_fixes.py`; LIM-*, DEC-06, METRIC-*, LAT-01, FDR-01, CENSUS-01). **Every row is
exploratory**: these are dev re-analyses chosen after the adversarial review
(`docs/review_paperA_adversarial_20260923.md` §F1, §F2, §F4, §F7), and none decides a registered verdict. Keys are
jq paths into the report named in the artifact column. Every value is first given as the JSON holds it (the
`jq -r` output, with the JSON's ASCII minus written − and a + on positive signed quantities); where the paper
prints a shorter form, "(printed …)" follows: pp values and their intervals at 2 decimals, means, rates and
p-values at 4 decimals, ratio intervals at 2 decimals (arm-mean intervals at 4). Unless a row says otherwise,
intervals are 95 % percentile cluster-bootstrap intervals, B = 10000, seed 20260924 (the channel
report's `settings` holds B = 10000, seed 20260924), scenario clustering first (19 clusters) and
task clustering second (57 clusters); pp values are arm minus comparator over (task_id, seed)
pairs; p is the two-sided scenario-bootstrap p, and a p of 0.0 means no resample crossed the threshold. DEC-06
continues the DEC family and COST-04 the COST family.

| claim_id | claim (one sentence) | artifact path | JSON key or line | status | figure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HO-01 | **In the pooled cap-81 prefix family the prefix leaves the executor no handoff in 4 of 171 episodes at m = 6, 54 of 171 at m = 9 and 100 of 171 at m = 11, and the zero-shot receiver's counts are the same.** Arms `hj17_prefix_c81_{bplus,zs}_m{6,9,11}_20260923` (seeds 1–2) pooled with `hj18_prefix_c81s3_{bplus,zs}_m{6,9,11}_20260924` (seed 3); 0 crashes in any input campaign [OBSERVED key `inputs.n_crash_total`]. "Silenced" (h = 0) is the prefix arm's `handoff_occurred = false`; a missing flag would count as silenced. Tailored, m6 / m9 / m11: `handoff_occurred = false` 4 / 54 / 100; executor never acted 4 / 43 / 83; `handoff_occurred = false` although the executor acted 0 / 11 / 17; flag missing 0 / 0 / 0 [OBSERVED key `handoff_only.bplus.m{6,9,11}.*`]. Zero-shot, same four counts in the same order: 4 / 54 / 100; 4 / 43 / 83; 0 / 11 / 17; 0 / 0 / 0 [OBSERVED key `handoff_only.zs.m{6,9,11}.*`]. The no-handoff counts match the review's recount from `events.jsonl` (docs/review_paperA_adversarial_20260923.md:37-42). "No handoff" and "executor never acted" differ for the reason ROB-12 records on the cap-25 source: the prefix can exhaust an episode after the executor has acted. **A11 (2026-09-24) →** The last sentence's explanation is wrong. "No handoff" (`handoff_occurred = false`) and "executor never acted" differ because the flag is `effective_m < n_source_actions` (src/sidekick/prefix_source.py:184), which is false whenever the source made no more than m executed actions, while the loop suppresses the live phase only when the replayed prefix is terminal (src/sidekick/systems/loop.py:743-756; `prefix_is_terminal` :172-180). The 11 (m = 9) and 17 (m = 11) flag-false episodes in which the executor acted are prefixes that did **not** exhaust the episode. At m = 11 all 17 sources stopped executing after 7 to 11 actions, never sent COMPLETE, and ran to the 40-step limit issuing only REPORT and ASK_PLANNER. The executor took control at step effective_m + 1 (HSTAR-14). The analysis indicator is now h* (HSTAR-01), under which silenced = "executor never acted" = 4 / 43 / 83 on both receivers (HSTAR-02); HO-02 to HO-NI-03 are superseded by HSTAR-03 to HSTAR-13 and kept as the flag sensitivity. ROB-12 gives the same explanation ("the prefix exhausted them, yet the executor still acted") for the cap-25 source. The mechanism is the same code, so it is very likely wrong there too [INFERRED], but the cap-25 source was not recomputed here. | `campaign/results/j17_depth_fixes_20260924.report.json` (`scripts/analysis/j17_depth_fixes.py`) | `handoff_only.{bplus,zs}.m{6,9,11}.{n,n_silenced,n_handoff_occurred_false,n_executor_never_acted,n_handoff_false_but_executor_acted,n_handoff_flag_missing}` | exploratory | — |

| NOISE-06 | **The registered 7 pp margin is the smallest non-inferiority margin that `docs/prereg_v1.md`'s power analysis resolved at ≥ 80 % power with N = 3 seeds over 168 tasks.** Simulated power was 0.86 (MCSE 0.007759) when the arm's and the reference's true rates were both 0.68. Fills **brief 63 / now 63** ("`docs/prereg_v1.md:88-95` power figures — power level and held-out task count"). Setup: latent-difficulty model calibrated on the measured two-seed discordance of 0.280702, 2,000 experiment replicates with 200 bootstrap draws each, `n_tasks` 168. The N = 3 cell is `cells[8]` (n_seeds 3, planner_true_rate 0.68, sidekick_true_rate 0.68). Power at the other margins in that cell: 0.591 at 5 pp and 0.9925 at 10 pp. The smallest margin at ≥ 80 % power is 7 at N = 3, 4 and 5, and 10 at N = 2 (`smallest_epsilon_pp_at_80_power`). Printed: **"≥ 80 % power (simulated 0.86) at N = 3 seeds over 168 held-out tasks"**. `docs/prereg_v1.md:92` quotes the same figures ("0.8600 ± 0.0078 MCSE", "168 tasks") [OBSERVED docs/prereg_v1.md:92]. The 168 tasks are the `test_normal` split size; the JSON records only the count [INFERRED from AppWorld's split sizes]. The simulated outcome is per-task success, i.e. TGC, which matches the paper's "smallest TGC margin" [INFERRED]. `docs/prereg_v1.md:94` adds that power at 7 pp falls to 0.4590 under the pessimistic mixture model [OBSERVED prereg line; `hj7_power_mixture.json` not re-read here]. | `campaign/results/hj7_power.json`; `docs/prereg_v1.md` | `cells[8].power_at_epsilon_pp["7"].{estimate,mcse}`, `n_tasks`, `reps`, `bootstrap_reps`, `smallest_epsilon_pp_at_80_power`; `docs/prereg_v1.md:88-95` | registered (prereg_v1 §2.3) | — |

| FDR-02 | **Benjamini–Yekutieli over every paired-contrast interval printed in the abstract and §1–§9 (60 intervals) keeps 13 at q ≤ 0.05 on the scenario-clustered bootstrap p, and 4 on the exact sign-flip p. None of the channel results survives: D0 at 171 pairs, C1 at 114, the cap change CEIL-07, the limit-as-0 sensitivity and the m = 11 DiD all fail.** Fills **brief 273 / now 289** and **brief 356 / now 372** ("BY adjustment over every interval the paper prints (R4.3)"). **The set.** Candidates are the paired-contrast intervals, one per distinct (contrast, population, metric). A paired contrast is a mean of per-pair differences on (task, seed), or on (scenario, seed) units for SGC. The class includes handoff-only and silenced subsets, the parts of the step-limit split, limit-as-0, chord residuals and per-episode DiD. Excluded: ratios, shares, slopes, arm means, anything printed without an interval, and the appendices. Of the 68 candidates, **60** are printed in the main text of `paper/preprint_dev_v2_20260924.md` sha256 `6089ca93c467202141aa85f285f667dd640e1dcd252ef2510af10ed9ee73eaa7` (303 lines before "## Appendix A"). Membership is found from the file at build time. By metric: 33 `goal_pass`, 21 TGC and 6 SGC. The 16 Table 6 NI cells are tested at −7.00 pp, as FDR-01 does; everything else is tested at 0. The main text holds 139 intervals. The 22 that no candidate accounts for are all in the excluded classes (listed at the key). The 8 other candidates are now printed only in the appendices: the T − N limit split (2), H2 vs the plan floor and vs advise_k10, the zs m11 chord, narrated untailored m9 → m11, narrated − executed tailored m11, and the DiD on TGC. **p.** Two-sided, scenario-clustered percentile bootstrap (`j10_report.bootstrap_pvalue`), 10,000 draws at seed 20260924. Companion: exact 19-scenario sign-flip. BY via `cluster_inference.by_fdr`, m = 60. **Bootstrap survivors, 13, with BY p.** H2 advise_k1 − prefix_m11 `goal_pass` 0.016848 (raw 0.0006). Depth, tailored m6 → 11: `goal_pass` all 0.00624, `goal_pass` handoff-only 0.023399, TGC all 0.0, TGC silenced 0.00624. Depth, untailored m6 → 11: `goal_pass` all 0.0, TGC all 0.0. Printed: "13 of the 60 intervals the main text prints survive BY at 0.05 (4 under the exact sign-flip test); D0, C1, CEIL-07 and the DiD do not (FDR-02)". **Reproduction.** Each of the 68 recomputed intervals matches its printed interval within 0.011 pp. ⚠ 13 of them match only at seed **20260915**, not 20260924: CEIL-07, C1 at 114 (`goal_pass` and TGC), the 4 H2 contrasts, the UF-07 chord and 5 narration contrasts. | CENSUS-02 | **The dev report JSONs hold at least 2,164 distinct paired contrasts with an interval: 8,917 contrast objects across 68 report files.** Fills **brief 376 / now 392** ("number of dev contrasts run (R4.4)"). **Rule** (verbatim at the key). Files: every `campaign/results/**/*.report.json` except those named `j10_*`, `j11_*` or `j12_*`, and this unit's own report. 3 files are excluded by name: `j10_a1_registered_dev_basis_20260924`, `j11_power_dev_20260924` and `j12_power_dev_20260924`. A contrast object is a JSON object that holds a paired-difference point (`diff_pp`, `diff`, `point_pp`, `diff_pp_scenario`, `residual_mean_pp` or `delta_pp`) and a key containing `ci95`, either at its own level or in a `scenario` / `task` sub-block. It can also be a `point` with a `ci95` key under a parent key matching one of `minus`, `_to_`, `delta`, `contribution`, `gain`, `did`, `diff` or `residual` (the report states the regex). Only the outermost such object counts. `n_objects` counts (file, key path) pairs: 8,917. `n_distinct` collapses objects whose (point, first interval), rounded to 4 dp, coincide: 2,164. **It is a lower bound.** Shares, ratios and tests printed without an interval are not counted. 13 of the 68 files contribute 0, among them the six `hj13_shape_*` hinge-fit reports, whose blocks hold β/τ intervals, not differences. The count also depends on when it is built: it includes `j17_planning_lit_20260924.report.json` (36 objects), which another unit wrote today. Printed: "at least 2,164 distinct paired contrasts (8,917 contrast objects in 68 dev report files; CENSUS-02)". | `campaign/results/j17_v2_fill_20260924.report.json` | `contrast_census.{rule,n_files,files_excluded_by_name,n_objects,n_distinct,per_file}` | exploratory (census) | — |

| TERM-01 | **Termination census comparable to Fan et al. (arXiv 2609.20804): definitions, and the no-op control that validates them.** An action is an event_type `action` of the acting actor. The executor's is written at `src/sidekick/systems/loop.py:598-605`; in planner_alone arms the planner's is written at `:998-1005`. A takeover move is the planner's (`:854-861`) and never counts. "Acted" means at least one action with `payload.kind == 'CODE'`: CODE and COMPLETE both reach `env.step` (`:1141-1143`), but COMPLETE ends the episode (`:1169-1170`). Prefix arms count only actions after the handoff, that is, step > the handoff report's `effective_m` (written at `src/sidekick/systems/prefix_handoff.py:183-189`). Categories, first rule that applies: no_handoff (prefix arm, `handoff_occurred` not true); limit; other (any other non-null error_type); stopped_before_acting (clean end, zero CODE actions); completed_after_acting [OBSERVED key `settings.definitions`]. Control: `dev_noop_complete_20260924` classifies 114/114 episodes as stopped_before_acting, share 1.0 (printed 1.0000), scenario and task [1.0, 1.0], median 1.0 steps, and all 114 actions are COMPLETE [OBSERVED key `term.arms.noop`]. Crashes dropped before classification: 0 in every census arm [OBSERVED key `provenance.*.n_crash`]. The handoff flag is read from the last `report` that carries `handoff_occurred`. j10's reader takes the last `report` from any actor; the two disagree on 0 episodes here [OBSERVED key `term.arms.*.n_handoff_flag_differs_from_j10_reader`]. | `campaign/results/j17_planning_lit_20260924.report.json` | `settings.definitions`; `term.arms.noop` | exploratory | termination census |

| HSTAR-01 | **The cap-81 prefix family's handoff indicator is now h* = "the executor took control". It is read from each prefix episode's own events, and it agrees with the loop's own terminal rule on all 1,026 prefix episodes (6 arms × 171). The `handoff_occurred` flag is kept as `h_flag`.** Rule: h* = 1 iff the last attempt in the prefix episode's `events.jsonl` holds an event that only the live loop writes, at step ≥ effective_m + 1. effective_m comes from the handoff record, a system `report` written at step effective_m (src/sidekick/systems/prefix_handoff.py:179-196). The live loop starts at `max(1, prefix.start_step)` = effective_m + 1 (loop.py:621, :753; prefix_source.py:178). Live events are: executor `action` / `error` / `ask` / `report` (loop.py:598-605, :546-580, :1053, :1087, :1112, :1130-1136); a system `error` without `exc_type` (limits :354-360, :760-766, :1176-1182); an environment `observation` / `error`, or a `local` `evaluate` (:786-792); and planner `intervention` / `action` / `handoff` / `error` / `action_review` (action_review_gate.py:77-88). Excluded: the final `evaluate` and `run_end` (:1196-1202, :1238-1245), which can sit above effective_m when the source prefix held REPORT steps, and the crash handler (:1206-1212) [OBSERVED scripts/analysis/handoff_control.py:1-60, :80, :118-141]. Validation, dev, both receivers. (1) h_flag = true ⇒ h* = 1 on 167/167, 117/117 and 71/71 at m = 6, 9, 11, with 0 violations. (2) On flag-false episodes, h* = NOT `prefix_is_terminal`, recomputed from the SOURCE episode with sidekick's own `build_handoff_prefix`, `last_observation_from_events` and `prefix_is_terminal`: m6 4 = 4 terminal + 0 live, m9 54 = 43 + 11, m11 100 = 83 + 17, 0 disagreements. These match the expected counts, and the equality holds on every episode (171/171 in all six arms). The handoff record agrees with the recomputed source on effective_m, n_source_actions, handoff_occurred and source campaign (0 mismatches in six arms), and the two receivers give identical keys. (3) A synthetic planless episode (effective_m 0) gives h* = 1. (4) A synthetic terminal episode (evaluate at step 10 > effective_m 9) gives h* = 0. Two further synthetic cases, a parse error first and a limit first, give h* = 1. `validation.all_hold` = true; 0 pre-loop limits; 0 undefined. | `campaign/results/j17_hstar_20260924.report.json` (`scripts/analysis/j17_hstar.py`) | `definition`, `validation.{v1_h_flag_true_implies_hstar,v2_flag_false_hstar_equals_not_source_terminal,v2_every_episode_hstar_equals_not_source_terminal,record_agreement,receivers_agree,v3_v4_synthetic,all_hold}` | exploratory | — |
